| 2026-09-24 | this commit | M5.0 | **(D49ag) The log recorded that a tool ran, never what it did — so a fold summarised a run and none of its work, and the model's memory between runs had the same hole.** D49aa fixed the fold's *rules* (it writes a row instead of clearing the log); this is the other half of the same fault, found by reading a real run rather than the code: **the rules were right and the data they read was empty.** A tool row was `{tool, phase}`, so both readers of the log — the fold's summariser and `requestFromLog`, which is the model's memory at the start of the next run — could see *that* work happened and never what it was. A real summary said so in as many words: *"No files read (contents not available in transcript)"*, a sentence about an empty prompt rather than about the model. **Measured on the founder's own 78-turn run** (`muejgc51j21i`, `~/.plato/runs/ff47b78d`): at the final turn **91,987 of 113,793 estimated tokens were tool results — 80.8%** — against 17.9% assistant and 0.2% user text, and the fold could see **0%** of the largest part. **The digest is the ARGUMENT, never the result, and that is the whole design:** `digestToolCall` takes the salient argument *by tool name* (path / grep pattern / shell command / URL) and never dumps `args`, because `args` for `write_file` is a whole file body and for `apply_diff` a whole diff — copying those in would rebuild the weight compaction exists to shed. Four decisions inside it: `planFold`'s `covered` now includes the tool rows **in log order** (when it happened is most of a summary), with the count the *user* is told still counting messages; the fold prompt moved to core as `buildFoldPrompt` **because the shell has no unit tests and that is the half that decides whether a fold records the work** (D49aa's argument for `applyFold`, applied to the part it missed); the request carries the tail's digests as a bracketed `[tool activity — what Plato ran]` turn, never as a sentence tacked onto the assistant's, so nothing arrives as words the model did not write; and **a row with no digest is skipped entirely**, so a log written before this produces a byte-identical request. Fixed the test. **Display is deliberately unchanged** — the digest is the model's memory, not a new chip. |

| 2026-09-24 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **"Say how you know" — the prompt now requires per-claim evidence, and the founder's constraint shaped it.** A real report fixed five things and was wrong about one, and **the failure was not the obvious one**: it cited `file:line` everywhere, so citations were never the gap — it read the code and drew a **wrong conclusion about what a library does** (what `SimpleDateFormat` emits from a `SSSSSSS` pattern), stated as fact. It was not dishonest either: its verification section was excellent and it flagged the ICU uncertainty by name. **The hole is that the caveat was REPORT-level while the claims were PER-CLAIM** — one flagged uncertainty sat beside an unflagged wrong one, with no way for the reader to tell them apart. The rule: every factual claim is **observed** (you ran it), **read** (you read the code — name the file and line), or **inferred**, and inference is allowed but **presenting it as observation is not**; a third-party library's edge-case behaviour is the high-risk case because that is where confident recall is wrong. **The founder rejected the shape I first proposed and was right:** I had suggested reaching for `act` rather than `edit` when verification matters, which makes accuracy a function of the mode — he said *"let's make verification etc not dependent on mode. accuracy is uncompromisable under any mode."* So it is written the other way: **a tool you do not have limits what you may CLAIM; it never licenses a guess**, holding in every mode — under my version `edit` would have been the mode whose findings were least trustworthy, which is backwards. Two smaller rules ride with it: a fix must **name the assumption it rests on** (this one was correct only if its strings came from a producer meaning fractional seconds, which the report never said), and for a **findings-then-fixes** task the findings are written out **before the first edit**. **Also (D49ad): `---` was rendering as three literal dashes in the prose** — the renderer emitted `<p>---</p>` where Claude Code draws a rule; a standalone `---`/`***`/`___` line is now a hairline `<hr>`, and the setext case is deliberately untouched because `Title` then `---` is an underline, not a rule. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **487/487** in 33 files (+12); render **14 suites / 295 assertions / 0 failures**; bundle + `node --check` PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **Falsified, reverted:** removing the thematic-break rule fails three markdown tests with the output printing `<p>---</p>` — the literal dashes are back. **Five prompt tests pin the new section** by its load-bearing sentences, including `This holds in every mode`. **A wrong reason of my own, caught by measuring:** I wrote `height:0` on the rule plus a comment saying Chrome otherwise paints it two pixels thick — **both wrong**, since the stylesheet is `box-sizing: border-box` and the border is the whole 1px; the declaration was removed and the comment corrected. That is the same failure this row is about, one screen after describing it. **Not verified:** no live run has used the new prompt section, so whether it changes model behaviour is untested — and the markdown rule is untested against a real model's separator style. |

| 2026-09-24 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **A reply is now readable — and the arithmetic backs the founder up.** He read Plato's first long report and said the headings were *"too small"* and everything *"jammed up"*. **The ladder was broken, not merely small:** body text is 13px and the headings were **15/14/13/12.5px**, so an `h3` was **the same size as the paragraph beneath it** and an `h4` was *smaller than the text it introduced*. Now **17/15.5/14/13.5px**. **"Jammed" was the space BELOW each heading — .25em** — so every section title sat on top of the text it introduced; above was `.7em` and fine. The asymmetry is the requirement: space above must dominate space below, or a heading binds to the text above it instead of to what follows. Below is now `.35–.45em`, above `.9–1.1em`; paragraphs `.35em → .5em`; list items `.15em → .3em`; and the reply gained a **1.5 line-height**, which for a long report does more than any of it. **A reply that OPENS with a heading gets no space above it** — otherwise it starts with a gap under its own name line. **One rule looked right and did nothing:** giving `strong` a brighter colour so identifiers stand out, which the render suite caught as being **exactly the body colour** because the body already uses `--vscode-editor-foreground`; deleted rather than fixed, since a rule that does nothing is worse than no rule — the next reader assumes it does something. **This row also carries the live confirmation of D49ab:** the run that produced this report **completed and wrote its summary**, which is the first evidence that removing the turn cap works on a real task. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **475/475**; render **14 suites / 288 assertions / 0 failures** including a new `summary` suite; bundle + `node --check` PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **Verified by measurement, not by looking:** the suite reads computed styles and asserts them **relative to the body size**, because that is what was broken — "headings are big enough" passes against 13px headings, "no heading is the same size as body text" does not. **Falsified, reverted:** the old ladder fails four assertions and reports `h3.size === 13 === body` — the founder's complaint as an assertion. **Not verified:** the readability was measured in the render harness at 760px, not read in a live panel, and no light/dark theme was applied (the harness has no VS Code theme variables). |

| 2026-09-24 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **The turn cap is gone — a run now ends when the model stops calling tools.** `MAX_TURNS = 40` is deleted, and the founder's question was not "raise it" but *"why is there even a cap"*. **The record answers badly: nobody asked for it.** It arrived inside **D22, marked "(delegated)"** — M1 choices left to the assistant — as one clause of the mode table: *"auto: everything auto-approved under a hard turn cap. All modes turn-capped."* The first half has a real reason (`auto` is the one mode with no click in the loop); **the second half is where the reasoning stops**, because `edit`/`act` gate every write behind an approval and `plan` cannot mutate, so in those modes the cap guarded nothing a human was not already guarding. **What that cost, from a real run:** in `edit` mode the run was stopped at turn 40 — `reason: "cap"` — with the third class untouched and no written review, **after the founder had already approved eleven writes.** It was not looping or burning: 10,442 output tokens across all 40 turns, 99.5% of input cached, 45.7k of a 1M window. **The user was the backstop and the cap still fired.** **Turns were the wrong unit anyway** — that run averaged ~261 output tokens a turn, while a thinking run can spend its whole total in three; a step count bounds neither money nor time. **The concrete that settles it:** two days earlier D49h built a *user-facing* `$/turns` budget and the founder removed it the same day as a capability nobody asked for, while its own text noted `MAX_TURNS` was the only bound left — so the bound he asked to remove went and the bound nobody asked for stayed. **`reason: 'cap'` now means exactly one thing, a truncated answer**, so `'[plato] turn cap reached; stopping.'` is deleted, the `auto` mode blurb no longer promises a cap as its backstop, and `background.ts` no longer tells the model it has 40 turns to spend. **A coverage finding:** deleting the cap broke **no test**, because **no test had ever described it** — three weeks of changes never touched it, which is why it survived unchallenged. **Deliberately NOT replaced:** in `auto` nothing now bounds a model that loops without concluding; the founder's read is that the runway fear is exaggerated and that Claude Code ships an auto mode with no limit, so the question is recorded in the backlog rather than answered with another invented number. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **475/475** in 33 files (+2, both new); render **13 suites / 273 assertions / 0 failures**; bundle + `node --check` PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **Falsified, reverted:** putting a 40-turn bound back fails *"keeps working past 40 turns while the model still calls tools"* with `expected 40 to be 56` — the exact stop the founder's run hit. **Two tests pin the replacement**, one running **55 turns** with the model still calling tools and asserting `complete`, the other asserting no output ever contains "turn cap". **Not verified:** no live run since, so a genuinely long run has not exercised the unbounded loop — and the `auto` gap is now real rather than theoretical. |

| 2026-09-23 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **Compacting the model's memory no longer deletes the user's conversation — the transcript is append-only.** The founder cancelled a run and watched his scrollback vanish, replaced by a summary. **The fold was scheduled in the run's `finally`**, so it ran on every way a run can end — success, error, or **cancel** — and its trigger is a message **count** (26 pairs), which his ~30-turn run passed the instant it stopped. **The damage was two lines:** `this.log.length = 0` followed by the summary plus the kept tail. **The reader was never wrong** — `historyFor` already found the newest summary row and built the request as *[summary] + what follows* — so the fix is **the deletion of a step, not a redesign**: the fold now WRITES a row at the cut and both readers handle that unchanged. **The root cause is a conflation worth naming:** the log is the user's transcript and the request is what the model sees; they were the same array, so a reasonable thing to do to a request — rebuild it from a summary — was a catastrophic thing to do to a transcript. **Plato is not the first to get this wrong:** Codex shipped the same defect and its fix proposal describes Plato's bug almost word for word (*"rewrites the live transcript… the user can no longer scroll up"*); in Codex's case the original text was still on disk and only the RENDER failed to backfill it. Nothing here needed to be lost. **The summary is no longer printed**: it is the model's memory, written to be terse and fact-only, and showing it put a machine note where the conversation already is. The marker says where memory now starts; the rows it covers are all still above it — the same conclusion the founder reached independently, since Claude Code does not print its summary either. **The display cap became honest too:** with the fold no longer deleting, `LOG_MAX` was the only thing left that could drop rows, and it did so silently. It now leaves a `trim` row saying how many went, and says so **differently when the cap took the summary**, because that is the model's memory going rather than the view. **Also recorded: `LOG_MAX` is 400 ENTRIES, not 400 messages** — every completed tool call is one row, so a run like the founder's spends ~70 of them, making 400 about five or six such runs. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **473/473** in 33 files (+15, a new `chatlog` suite); render **13 suites / 273 assertions / 0 failures** including a new `transcript` suite (neither marker was covered before); bundle + `node --check` PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **Falsified twice, each reverted:** implementing `applyFold` as the discarded rewrite fails *"writes a row without removing any row from the log"* — the log shrinks from **33 rows to 25**; printing the summary again fails *"the summary text is NOT printed into the transcript"* (the assertion output shows the summary text back in the transcript). **Two of my own new tests were wrong first** and the code was right: I mis-counted how many messages a cap leaves, and I asserted the fold would not re-cover rows a previous summary covered — which it must, because those rows are now *kept* rather than deleted. **Then CONFIRMED IN PRODUCTION the same day:** a real 40-turn run on the Android repo (transcript `mueg6d3yh7ea`) triggered a fold of 28 messages and the founder **could still scroll back to his original message** — the transcript survived, which is this entry's whole claim. **The run itself is also the evidence for the next problem:** 40/40 turns, `reason: "cap"`, only **10,442 output tokens** across the whole run (~261/turn) and 99.5% of its 1.35M input tokens were cache reads, at 45.7k of a 1M window with **no in-run compaction**. The model behaved well — it reverted its own wrong fix (*"my normalizeMilliseconds helper is dead code… the honest correction: remove that helper"*), refused to ship what it could not verify (*"I can't run a JVM to test"*), tracked its own side effects, and caught its own wasted step — and **the cap cut it at roughly two-thirds, before the written review, leaving the third class untouched.** Also found: `grep`'s literal default silently returned nothing for a regex-shaped pattern and the model nearly concluded a live method was dead code; the price table does not know `claude-sonnet-5`, so the run's cost reads **$0** for 1.35M input tokens; ~5–7 turns went to patches that did not land (whitespace mismatch, plus two identity edits the tool reported honestly as `no-op`); and the run log records tool names and phases but **not arguments**, so a transcript cannot say which files a run touched. **The fold's notice still described the old behaviour** (*"Compacted 28 earlier messages into a summary"*, printed directly under those 28 messages) and now reads *"Summary added for 28 earlier messages. Nothing was removed."* — a mechanism and the sentence reporting it are two things. **Not verified:** the summary still cannot see tool calls or results, so a tool-heavy run still summarises to nothing. |

| 2026-09-23 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **The tick boxes are gone: every model a provider returns is eligible, and every one of them shows in the chat picker.** D49x fixed the symptom — a chat running a model the founder had unticked — by asking whether a model is *offered*. This removes the **state**, on his call: *"user can't uncheck or opt out of model (that's confusing)."* **The boxes were an opt-out, not a gate, and that reframes the size of the change:** "Fetch models" already wrote the whole fetched list as available, so the default was always "everything works" and the boxes could only ever narrow it — his own screenshot is exactly that narrowing (nine fetched, one ticked). **What the narrowing cost, beyond his bug:** the chat is not the only place a model reference is stored — **every Duo and Academy member stores one**, validated only for *shape* (`<connection>:<model>`), never against what is available. Unticking a model could therefore leave a council member pointing at a name the key refuses, and that 403 would land **mid-run, after the doer had already done work**. D49x covered the chat; nothing covered the members. With no opt-out, no stored reference can be out of the offered set, so the class is gone rather than guarded. **Two config fields became one, and the read rule is the migration:** a connection carried `models` (ticked subset) and `allModels` (fetched pool), and the boxes were the only thing that could make them differ; `resolveOfferedModels` reads the **wider** list first and falls back to the narrower only when no fetch has happened, so a config narrowed before this change offers everything immediately — **nothing is rewritten in the user's settings, and no migration step exists**. Reading the narrow one would have preserved the fault, which is why the order matters. **The Settings list is now a reference, not a control**: it says `Models — N available` rather than `N of M active` (that wording promised *active*, which is why his screenshot read as a bug rather than a preference), and its inert rows lost the pointer cursor and the hover, because a row that highlights claims to be clickable. **D49x's rule is NOT superseded** — with nothing to untick it now covers only what nothing can prevent: the endpoint withdrew a model, or the connection was deleted. **The founder's next idea — drag-to-reorder the list — is deliberately NOT built**: it is a capability rather than a repair, and it is recorded as a backlog item with its four unsettled points. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **458/458** in 32 files (+4 for `resolveOfferedModels`); render **12 suites / 259 assertions / 0 failures**; bundle + `node --check` PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **Falsified three times, each reverted:** putting a single tick box back fails the "no model can be ticked out of availability" assertion (**boxes: 4**), the OK/Cancel assertion (**buttons: 4**), and the inert-row assertion (**cursor: "pointer"**); rewording the summary back to the active-subset phrasing fails the summary assertion. **Not verified:** no live run, and **the migration is proven against fixtures, not against the founder's real config** — his settings.json still holds a narrowed `models` beside a wider `allModels`, so the first thing to check is that his picker lists all nine. |
| 2026-09-23 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **Bug 3 is solved — and it was never the dropdown's fault. A model the founder had UNCHECKED in Settings was still the model the chat would run.** His screenshot: `ornith-1.5-35b-a3b` unticked in Providers, that same model in the chat's picker marked current, labelled *"not in the fetched list"*. **Two causes behind one symptom.** **(1) The rule asked the wrong question.** `effectiveModel()` kept the preferred ref whenever its **connection** existed — which a deselected model satisfies, because the connection is still there. So the chat stayed on a model the user had disabled, and the run would have sent it — not cosmetic, because **a key scoped to a model list answers 403 for a name it does not allow**, the exact error he had already reported. The rule is now a pure function, `pickEffectiveModel`, which asks whether the model is **offered**; a sibling on the **same connection** is preferred over the first available model, because losing a model is not a reason to change vendors, prices and keys. **(2) The picker's choice never survived a reload.** The composer and doer selects are the only model controls in the product, and a click wrote memory only, so **every reload reverted to `plato.model`** — a setting with **no UI whatsoever** (its one handler is never posted by any view), editable only by hand in `settings.json`. A hand-set value from an earlier session is precisely what kept reappearing, which is why it looked intermittent: it came back after each window reload. The pick now writes that setting, making the doer row's help text true — *"change it here or in the composer — it is the same setting"* — which it was not. **The fallback row stays**: a current model no connection offers is still shown honestly, so pooled and custom names keep working. **Accepted, stated plainly: unchecking the model a chat is using moves that chat to another enabled model on the same connection, silently.** Every trigger is the user's own action or a stale-config correction, and the model chip is always visible; a notice is one line if he wants it. **Also: the free-text answer to a Plato question is now a textarea that grows to three lines and then scrolls**, instead of a one-line input that scrolled sideways while you were still typing. The **cap** is the real requirement, not the growth — the card carries its own Submit button, so an unbounded box would push the button off the card. Height is measured from the box's own `scrollHeight` clamped at three resolved line-heights, with `line-height` **stated** in CSS because an unset one resolves differently per font. The prediction that mattered: a hidden element measures **zero**, and the box starts hidden behind the *Others* radio — so an answer restored by **Back** measured as a 0px sliver until the fit was moved to where the row is already in the document. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **454/454** in 32 files (+6 for `pickEffectiveModel`); render **12 suites / 255 assertions / 0 failures**, including a new `question` suite; bundle + `node --check` on the inlined script PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **Reproduced at the logic level before fixing:** the old rule (keep the ref if its connection exists) fails exactly the two new assertions and returns **`litellm:ornith-1.5-35b-a3b`** — the founder's own screenshot, in a test. **Falsified four times, each reverted:** the old connection-only rule fails the two selection tests; deleting the three-line clamp grows the box to **5 and 6 lines** (the button-off-the-card state) and fails three assertions; removing the fit on restore collapses the restored box to **0.99 lines** and fails one; removing the fit on input leaves the box at **0.99 lines**, scrolling, and fails three. **Not verified:** no live run — the **403 that motivated this is not re-tested against LiteLLM**, and the reload-persistence fix needs a real window reload to confirm end to end. |
| 2026-09-23 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **Two fixes: Plato's invented output ceiling is gone, and a settings payload can no longer drag the user out of the chat.** **(D49w) The output ceiling.** `maxTokens` was `Math.min(16384, ...)` — a 16,384 cap applied even when the model's real limit was known and larger. It dates from 0.7.6, where it was a **raise** from 4,096 that fixed runs ending with no answer; it is now what causes them. The founder's last run ramped **2,559 → 4,783 → 8,201 → 16,384 exactly**, spent the whole budget thinking and produced no visible text. The cap is deleted: a known model gets its own limit. `MAX_TURNS` still bounds the run and `resolveMaxOutput` still falls back to a conservative 8,192 when nothing knows the model, so an UNKNOWN model behaves exactly as before — what changed is that a **known** limit is used rather than overridden. **The consequence, stated plainly: one turn can now cost more.** That is the model's own ceiling and the model the user chose. **(D49v) The chat could be yanked into Settings.** Every `settings` payload called `showSettings`, and the host sends one after **any** connection change — including a model fetch that lands a network round trip after the user has moved on. Adding a provider is exactly that sequence, which is why it looked tied to the first model pick. **My earlier fix (D49u) addressed the wrong half**: it corrected the stale `hasConnections` flag, which was a real bug and a different one. The view now follows only a payload the host marks as **the reply to a request**; every other payload is a background refresh that renders in place. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **448/448** (one pre-existing test asserted the 16,384 ceiling while its own name said "the full default" — updated to 64000, the model's real limit); render **11 suites / 236 assertions / 0 failures**. **Reproduced before fixing, both:** the unprompted-payload case fails on the old code (the view moves), and the D49t wiring test fails if the ceiling is restored. **Falsified both, reverted:** restoring `Math.min(16384, …)` fails exactly the two cap tests; restoring the unconditional `showSettings` fails the new view assertion. **Not verified:** no live run since — the next one is the evidence. |
| 2026-09-23 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **Bug 2 solved, and it was a SECURITY bug: the environment was choosing Plato's endpoint and adding a credential to every Anthropic request.** The founder's complete error was `anthropic HTTP 403` carrying a **LiteLLM** body — the Anthropic adapter, talking to LiteLLM. Cause, confirmed from the SDK's own source: `new Anthropic({ baseURL = readEnv('ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL'), apiKey = readEnv('ANTHROPIC_API_KEY'), authToken = readEnv('ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN') })`. Plato passed **only `apiKey`**, so both other defaults came from the ambient environment — and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` is set on this machine to the LiteLLM proxy. **Every "Anthropic" connection had been going to LiteLLM**, whatever its settings said. **The second half is worse than the first.** `authToken` also defaulted from `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` — also set here — so every Anthropic request carried an **ambient credential Plato never chose**, as an extra `Authorization` header, in addition to its own key from SecretStorage. Plato has been attaching a token that is not its own to requests it did not intend to send where they went. **Why it looked so confusing:** the model LIST beside the connection was fetched correctly, because discovery always passed a base URL explicitly. So the Settings page showed genuine Anthropic models at real context windows while the runs went elsewhere and were refused. Two surfaces disagreeing about which server they were talking to. **The fix:** the adapter passes `baseURL` and `authToken: null` **explicitly** — a connection may still name its own endpoint, which is configuration the user can see, but there is no invisible third source. `createProvider` passes the base URL through instead of dropping it, and the three host call sites hand over each connection's own URL rather than an OpenAI-shaped default. **Falsified:** reverting to `new Anthropic({ apiKey })` fails exactly the three new tests. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **448/448** (+3); render **11 suites / 233 assertions / 0 failures**; bundle + `node --check` PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **Not verified:** no live call was made to either endpoint — the assertions are on what Plato PASSES to the SDK, which is precisely the defect and the only thing Plato controls. **Bug 3 (stale names in the dropdown) is still untouched** — it is the deliberate D39/D47h selection-preserve, and needs a reproduction first. |
| 2026-09-23 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **Bug 1 of three: adding a provider then opening the model dropdown threw the user back into Settings.** The chat-side "are there any providers" flag was set **only** by the `models` and `restore` payloads — but adding a provider sends a **`settings`** payload. So the flag stayed `false`, and the model button routes to onboarding when it believes there are no connections, so the click bounced straight back out of the chat the user had just returned to. `showSettings` now derives the flag from the payload's own `connections`. **Reproduced before fixing:** a new render case walks the exact sequence (no providers → add one → back to chat → click the model button) and fails on the old code, which posts `openSettings`. **The other two reports are NOT fixed, and the honest reason is that I could not pin them from the outside.** The second — a run still going to LiteLLM after selecting an Anthropic model — has several candidate causes and no way to tell them apart from an error message that names the key's allowed models but never says what Plato actually asked for. **So the error now says where the request went and which model it named** (`Sent to litellm (https://…) for model "claude-fable-5".`). That makes the class of bug self-evident on the next attempt instead of needing another round of guessing. The third — the dropdown keeping stale model names — is the **deliberate** selection-preservation from D39/D47h; it needs a reproduction before it is touched, because the obvious fix is the thing that feature exists to prevent. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **445/445** (unchanged, shell-only); render **11 suites / 233 assertions / 0 failures**; bundle + `node --check` PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **A TS error caught a D49u mistake worth remembering:** the explanatory comment inside `webview.ts` used **backticks** around a word, which closes the file's one giant template literal — HANDOFF gotcha 1, hit again, caught by `tsc` rather than by a render suite because nothing renders that path. |
| 2026-09-22 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **The Settings model list is the one that got the single-column treatment — I fixed the wrong list first.** The founder's screenshot showed Settings → Providers still as a two-column grid of bare, truncated names. **I had changed the composer's model popover instead**, and reported it as done: two different surfaces, both "the model list", and I picked the one I had just been reading rather than the one he was looking at. That is the same failure as the earlier `.card:last-of-type` fallback — assuming the thing in front of me was the thing meant. The Settings list is now one model per line, name over its context window, scrolling — and the windows are plumbed through the SAME `resolveMaxContext` rule the run and the composer use, so no two surfaces can quote different numbers for one model. A model nothing reported still says **context unknown** rather than inventing a figure. **Separately, the run timer moved into the status bar.** It used to be a second chip in the header beside Solo / new, while the bar at the end of the conversation separately said *what* Plato was doing — two places to answer one question. The bar now carries both: the status text, and the seconds ticking on its right. On finish it reports `✓ done in Ns` in the done colour with the timer cleared. **`lastAct` became write-only the moment the timer stopped reading it, and was removed** — 8 assignments, no readers. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **445/445** in 32 files (unchanged — this is shell-only); render **11 suites / 230 assertions / 0 failures**, with new assertions in `providers` for the Settings list and a new `runstatus` suite that drives a REAL send, because `busy`/`runT0` are set by the composer and by nothing else. **Falsified three times:** restoring the two-column grid fails the one-column assertion (the output shows `columns: "298px 298px"`); putting the header chip back and cutting the timer off from the bar fails three assertions between them. **Not verified:** the Settings list was exercised at 720px in a render harness, not in a live panel with a real 200-model OpenRouter connection. |
| 2026-09-22 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **The model's real context window now reaches the run, not just the header — and the picker shows it.** The founder's call: *"we can't be shipping Plato with an 8k context window when models report their sizes"*, and he was right — but the audit found **Plato already fetched them and never used them.** Two places answered "how big is this model": the header resolved `maxContextFor(model) ?? poolEntry?.maxContext ?? null`, while **the run asked the catalog alone** — so every LiteLLM group and self-hosted model fell to `UNKNOWN_CONTEXT_FLOOR` (8192) and compaction tripped after three files. A wire that was never connected, not a missing capability. **Step 1 — the wire.** `LoopOptions.advertised` carries what the connection reported; both the ring and the run now resolve through ONE rule, `resolveMaxContext` / `resolveMaxOutput`. The same disconnect hid the OUTPUT cap: `maxOutputFor` also fell back to 8192, which a reasoning model exhausts on thinking alone — **which is the empty-turn condition that caused the D49s crash.** So one missing wire produced both symptoms. **Step 2 — discovery happens on its own.** It used to require pressing "Fetch models", so a connection never refreshed had no numbers at all. `ensureModelPool` now runs before a run, bounded at 5s, swallowing failures, and refreshes a pool older than 24h — a rotated key or a withdrawn model must not leave a stale number in place. **Step 3 — the fields.** A **trap removed first**: `max_tokens` was accepted as a context window, and it is the OUTPUT cap — a proxy echoing `max_tokens: 4096` had Plato record 4096 as the model's whole memory. Harmless while the number only decorated a label; a direct fault the moment it drives compaction. Added vLLM's `max_model_len` (the window actually served) and **LiteLLM's `/model/info`, which is where LiteLLM reports sizes at all** — `/v1/models` does not carry them. And when two numbers disagree, **the smaller wins** (`minNum`), because the two ways to be wrong are not symmetric: too small summarises early and still works, too large overflows — and on the common local servers that **truncates silently**. **The UI he asked for.** Model rows are now one per line — name, with the window as a subtitle — and the list scrolls; the row is a modifier so the mode and slash pickers keep their own layout. A million reads **1M, never 1000k** (all three formatters). | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **445/445** in 32 files (+16); render **10 suites / 207 assertions / 0 failures** including a new `modellist` suite; bundle + `node --check` PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **Falsified four times, each reverted:** restoring the `max_tokens` trap fails the trap test; ignoring `opts.advertised` fails both wiring tests; dropping the millions unit makes the picker print **`1000k context`** — the founder's exact complaint, caught by the suite; reverting the row to inline meta fails the subtitle assertions. **Not verified:** `/model/info` has never been exercised against a live LiteLLM — the shape handling and the soft-fail are pinned by tests, the endpoint itself is not. **Steps 4 (a user-declared window) and 5 (Ollama/llama.cpp runtime values) are NOT built** — the founder is undecided, and neither is needed until discovery fails for a setup that matters. |
| 2026-09-22 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **The second real run found a DIFFERENT crash: an empty assistant turn was sent to the wire.** The re-run got much further — nine turns, including the model noticing a literal grep and redoing it with regex — then died with `openai HTTP 400: Invalid assistant message: content or tool_calls must be set` (DeepSeek via LiteLLM). **That the failure moved proves the D49r fix worked.** **Root cause:** a turn cut by the output cap that produced **no visible content** was still recorded as an assistant message. A reasoning model can spend the whole output budget on `reasoning_content`, which this adapter never reads, so the turn arrives with `content: []`. The loop then pushed its *"continue exactly where you left off"* instruction and sent the pair — and the assistant turn carried neither text nor a tool call, which the wire contract forbids. **The semantic fault underneath is worse than the wire error:** there was nothing to continue from. Asking a model to resume where it left off, when it left off nowhere, is a request that cannot succeed. **The fix:** auto-continue now requires `res.content.length > 0`; an empty capped turn ends the run with the cap note, which is what actually happened. **Reproduced before fixing**, and the reproduction caught a fault in my own test: it held the live `messages` array, which the loop keeps mutating after the call returns, so it was asserting against post-run state rather than against what was sent. Snapshotting the array made it measure the real thing. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **429/429** in 32 files (+2); render **9 suites / 191 assertions / 0 failures**; bundle + `node --check` PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **Falsified:** reverting the condition fails exactly the 2 new tests, and the earlier truncation tests (which carry text) still pass — so the fix did not break auto-continue for the case it was built for. **Not verified:** no live provider call, so neither fix has been exercised against a real DeepSeek round trip. |
| 2026-09-22 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **The first real run found a crash: compaction could strand tool results, and a strict provider rejects that outright.** Plato's first ever run on a real project (Solo, `edit`, the HCAD Android repo) died on turn 3 with `openai HTTP 400: Messages with role 'tool' must be a response to a preceding message with 'tool_calls'`. **Root cause: `compactionSplit` was a blind slice by count.** When the cut landed inside a tool exchange, the kept tail began with tool RESULTS whose assistant message (carrying the `tool_calls`) had been summarised away — so the request contained a `tool` message with nothing to answer. **It is not an OpenAI-only fault:** Anthropic enforces the same rule for `tool_result` blocks, so any compaction landing mid-exchange would have broken there too. The split now walks the cut back until the kept tail can stand alone, keeping FEWER messages than asked: a valid request matters more than a target count. **The trigger is a second, separate issue and is NOT yet fixed:** the model ref is a **LiteLLM group name** (`Auto-Act`), which is not in Plato's catalog, so `maxContextFor` returns null and `shouldAutoCompact` falls back to `UNKNOWN_CONTEXT_FLOOR` = **8,192 tokens** — compaction therefore trips at ~6,553 tokens, which three Java files plus a handful of tool results blow past by turn 3. A DeepSeek-class model behind LiteLLM holds far more. So compaction fires far too early AND, until this fix, produced an invalid request every time it did. **No setting exists for a model's context window**; the loop comment records that trusting a `/models` payload for this was deliberately rejected (routes report stale windows). Ways to close it are a decision, not a patch — see the PROGRESS backlog. | `tsc -b --force` clean; vitest **427/427** in 32 files (+3, reproducing the crash first: the property test sweeps EVERY keep count and fails on the old code at the counts that land inside an exchange); render **9 suites / 191 assertions / 0 failures**; bundle + `node --check` on the inlined script PASS; VSIX packaged and installed. **Reproduced before fixing, not reasoned about:** the test was written against the unfixed split and failed for exactly the predicted reason, then passed. `compactionSplit` has one caller (`compactMessages`), so the change is contained. **Not verified:** no live provider call was made — the fix is proven against the message shapes the loop builds, not against a real DeepSeek round trip. |
| 2026-09-21 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **Simplification: the domain lists are DELETED and redirects are followed with each hop validated — Phase 5 is now DONE.** The founder read the previous row and asked the question I should have asked when I built them: *"are we over-engineering this?"* **Yes.** `plato.net.allow`/`net.deny` shipped enforced, with **no panel UI**, and were being carried as the one outstanding piece of Phase 5 (D49q). **The argument that killed them:** `web_fetch` asks for approval in **every** mode, `auto` included — the founder's own posture — so egress never happens unattended, and an allow list **could only ever subtract**. | 2026-09-17 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **D49h–i — Phase 1 complete: a run budget, and a cancel that actually stops the call in flight.** **(D49h) The only thing bounding a run was `MAX_TURNS = 40`** — cost was computed and displayed and nothing acted on it, so a runaway run could spend without limit and the user had no control that said "stop at $2". A run now stops cleanly once it has spent its `usd` budget or used its `turns` budget. **The check runs at the END of a COMPLETED turn, and both reasons matter:** a turn's tools always finish, so a multi-file edit is never cut off half-applied — cutting mid-turn leaves the repo in a state neither the model nor the user planned — and `turn` at that point is a turn that actually *ran*, so the reported count stays honest rather than over-reporting by one. The first turn always runs even against an already-exceeded budget, so a budget can never produce a run that did nothing. It is a **guard rail, not an accountant**: `usd` is compared against the accumulated *estimate* from the display-only price table, and the setting says so. **`0` means no cap and the live label says `no cap` in words, never "$0.00"** — a cap of zero and no cap are opposites, and the bare number cannot tell them apart; the host clamps a negative or non-finite value to 0 too, so a bad setting cannot silently stop every run before it starts. **(D49i) `gate()` can only refuse at a safe point, so a provider call already in flight was unstoppable** — a hung request hung the run with no way out, and Phase 2's streaming makes a stalled stream more likely, which is why this landed before it. `RunController.cancel()` now aborts an `AbortController` whose signal is threaded through `TurnRequest` to both HTTP layers (the SDK's request options for Anthropic, `fetch`'s `init` for OpenAI). **The trap that made this more than plumbing:** an aborted request and a transport failure are *indistinguishable at the error level* — both are `ProviderError` with status `null` — and status `null` is deliberately **retryable** (D49f), so the naive version would answer a user's cancel with a fresh round of retries. The retry policy now takes the signal and refuses to retry once it aborts, and the loop's catch checks `signal.aborted` to tell a deliberate stop from a real failure — reporting *"provider request failed"* for a cancel the user asked for would be a lie about what happened. **Also: two visual defects in the new row that every assertion passed and a screenshot caught in one glance** — the live label sat hard against its field while the row above right-aligned its own, so the two rows of one section disagreed about where the value goes (`margin-left:auto`); and the section still read "Context — compaction" while now holding a budget, so it reads **"Context — limits"**. | `tsc -b --force` clean; `pnpm -r test` **274/274** in 26 files (260 + **14 new**: 7 `budget.test.ts`, 7 `abort.test.ts`); `pnpm run render` — **seven suites, 182 assertions, 0 failures, exit 0** (the new `budget` suite is the 7th). **The `budget` suite DRIVES the control rather than rendering it**: it types into the field, fires the real `change` event, and reads the `set-budget` message back off `window.__posted` — so it covers the handler wiring, not just markup, and proves `0` and a negative read "no cap". **Falsifiable, three probes at once:** making `budgetBreach` return null fails **5 budget tests** (`expected 40 to be 2` — i.e. the run falls through to the 40-turn cap); disabling the retry abort guard fails the abort-retry test; disabling the loop's abort branch fails the cancelled-not-failed test. | 2026-09-17 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **D49f–g — Phase 1 of the agent capability plan, part 1: a transient failure no longer loses the run, and the stable prompt prefix is cached.** **(D49f) There was NO retry anywhere in the repo.** A single 429 — or one dropped socket — ended the run and discarded every turn already completed, which on a long agentic run is both the most expensive failure mode and the most common one. The worker's provider call now retries `429` / `5xx` / a transport failure with exponential backoff and full jitter. **Any other `4xx` is deliberately NOT retried** — a bad key (401), an unknown model (404) or a malformed body (400) fails identically the second time, so a retry only burns time and money; this is precisely why `ProviderError` carries the HTTP status (D49d) rather than a formatted string. The provider's own `Retry-After` wins over the computed backoff when present. Three limits stop a retry becoming a hang: a bounded attempt count (4), a bounded per-wait delay (20s), and a total elapsed budget (90s) checked **before** each wait so the policy never starts a sleep that would overshoot it. Every retry is announced — *"rate limited — retrying in 4s (attempt 2 of 4)"* — and seconds are rounded **up**, because a 500 ms wait reported as "0s" reads like a bug. **Applied at the worker turn only, on purpose:** compaction and the observer call `completeTurn` directly and both already degrade gracefully (a failed compaction continues with a notice; a failed observation degrades to `noop`), so neither can lose a run and a second retry budget would only hide how long a turn really takes. **(D49g) Prompt caching.** The adapter marks the **stable prefix** — the system prompt plus the tool schemas, identical on every turn of a run — as cacheable, with one breakpoint on the system block and one on the **last** tool (which is what caches the whole array). **A deviation from the D49 sketch, recorded rather than silently taken:** the plan had `TurnRequest.system` becoming a block array; it ships as one optional `cache?: boolean` with the adapters building their own wire shape — same benefit, no widening of a canonical type, and the loop, compaction and observer are all untouched. **The load-bearing part is that `inputTokens` is now normalised to a true TOTAL**, because Anthropic reports `input_tokens` **net** of cache while OpenAI reports `prompt_tokens` **gross**; mapping either straight through would make the context ring and the cost disagree between providers. The cost model had to change in the same breath: Anthropic bills a cache write at **1.25×** and a read at **0.1×**, so leaving `estimateCost` alone would have made the displayed cost **fall** exactly when the real bill rose. The price table now carries per-model cache multipliers and splits the total into fresh, written and read — which supersedes the standing "cache discounts not modeled" limitation for the models in the table. **Only the stable prefix is cached, not the conversation:** compaction *replaces* the history, so a history cache would be thrown away and re-written at the premium rate every time it fired. | `tsc -b --force` clean; `pnpm -r test` **260/260** in 24 files (231 + **29 new**: 17 `retry.test.ts`, 7 `cache.test.ts`, 5 cache-pricing cases in `cost.test.ts`); `pnpm run render` — six suites, **160 assertions, 0 failures, exit 0**. **`cache.test.ts` is the first test `AnthropicNativeProvider` has ever had** — the plan flagged that gap before phase 2 rewrites the file; the SDK is mocked so the request body can be inspected, which is the only way to prove where the breakpoints actually land. **Both halves proven falsifiable.** Making `isRetryable` return false fails **exactly the 8 tests** that assert retrying, with the 9 asserting non-retry behaviour (401, the elapsed budget, parsing) still passing. Setting the cache multipliers to 1 and removing both `cache_control` sites fails **exactly the 5 tests** that assert caching, with the other 13 still passing. One pre-existing test was updated rather than worked around: `providers.test.ts` asserted the exact `TurnUsage` shape, and D49g widened it with the cache figures — an endpoint reporting no cache must still yield an explicit `0`, not `undefined`. **Not verified:** no live provider call was made, so the retry path and the cache saving have unit-level but not network-level evidence; the actual cache saving is unmeasurable here and must be read off the panel. |
| 2026-09-17 | (this commit) | M5.0 | **D49a–e — Phase 0 of the agent capability plan: trust and correctness.** An audit of the tool surface, the provider contract and the render harness against what a modern coding agent does found **four capability holes** (no streaming, no image input, no web access, no retry on a failed provider call) and **three live defects**. This phase fixes the defects and makes the evidence loop honest. **(D49a) The render suites could not fail.** `check()` only logged, and **no suite set `process.exitCode`** — `process.exit` appeared exactly once in the whole directory, at `run.mjs:46`. So a suite printing `FAIL: 12` exited **0**, `run.mjs` counted it as passed on `r.status !== 0`, and the run ended **ALL RENDER SUITES PASS**. Every such line in this file's history is weaker evidence than it reads: it proves no uncaught exception fired — which is what `checkPageClean` legitimately catches — but nothing about whether the assertions held. Worse, `modelsync` and `panels` did not import `plib.mjs` at all: they launched their own browser and kept their own local `let fail`, so a fix reading `counters.fail` would have silently reported **0** for both. All six suites now import the shared `check`/`page()`/`finish()`, which removes the class rather than the instance. `run.mjs` also builds **once** per run now (`PLATO_RENDER_PREPARED=1`) instead of once per suite. **(D49b) `write_file` could not overwrite anything.** The declared schema said "refused for existing non-empty files", but the code tested **existence**, not emptiness — and `apply_diff` rejects an empty SEARCH, so an existing **empty** file was unreachable by *every* write tool and the only route was `shell`, which plan and edit deny. Now: an existing empty file is always writable; a non-empty file needs an explicit `overwrite: true`, which keeps the guard (a free overwrite would make the surface's one destructive operation un-approved in `auto`) and loses no capability. The safety claim for that flag is only true if the user sees what is lost, so `buildApprovalPreview` now returns a real diff against the existing content instead of showing only the incoming text — before this the argument was simply false. **(D49c) `read_file` cut at 60,000 chars silently** — a larger file could not be read at all. Now `offset`/`limit` with an explicit marker saying which lines came back and how to continue; the window is bounded by **both** a line and a char budget, because one minified line can be megabytes; and the whole-file path stays **byte-exact**, because the model builds `apply_diff` SEARCH strings out of this text and a lost trailing newline becomes a spurious "SEARCH not found". **(D49d) One provider error shape.** The two adapters disagreed — the OpenAI-compatible path threw a formatted string, `anthropic-native.ts` had **no error handling at all** — and the shell then discarded whichever it got with a bare `catch {}`, showing one fixed sentence ("Check the API key and network") for a 401, a 429 and a dead socket alike. Both adapters now throw a typed `ProviderError` carrying the status, which is what Phase 1's retry policy has to branch on, and `describeError()` surfaces it. **(D49e) The dead M0 pair removed** — `runtime/solo.ts` and `providers/anthropic.ts` referenced only each other, were reachable solely through one `index.ts` re-export, and had no caller in `packages/vscode`; grepped before deleting. Its streaming call was the repo's only working one, so it is preserved at `136564b` for Phase 2. This is phase 0 of 7; the roadmap and the three founder decisions (subagents deferred; the edit surface extended additively rather than replaced; web access as a new tool-kind that always asks, including in `auto`) are **DECISIONS D49**. | `tsc -b --force` clean; `pnpm -r test` **231/231** in 22 files (219 + **12 new**, in the first test file the tool executor has ever had); `pnpm run render` — **160 assertions, 0 failures, exit 0**; bundle + `extract-inline.cjs` + `node --check` PASS; VSIX packaged @0.25.0 and installed. **Falsifiability proven twice.** (1) A probe importing `finish` with one false assertion **exits 1**; with a true one it exits 0. The box now auto-fits its text plus **exactly one line**, re-fitting on input and on panel-width change, and measures a **hidden mirror** of the text instead of the textarea's own `scrollHeight` (which over-reports: 5.01 lines for text needing 4 — that rounding was producing a whole extra line). It was the only `danger` call in the file, so nothing else exposed it. `.btn-danger` is now the real rule (24px transparent square, muted glyph, red on hover) and the variant name **is** the class name, so it cannot silently fall back. **(3) The two walls now match (founder chose "grey out").** Remove was *not drawn* at the floor, so going 3→2 made two other buttons vanish during a destructive action. The tooltip carries the reason, the click is swallowed in JS, and the footer states the range permanently (`3 of 5 philosophers · minimum 2`) instead of narrating a control. Duo has one fixed philosopher — not a bounded list — so it has no `✕` (`showRemove:false`). **A 1,000-character `role` cap** (`ROLE_MAX_CHARS`, core) is enforced in **both** parsers (`role is 1001 characters — the maximum is 1000`), both JSON schemas (`maxLength: 1000`) and the box (`maxlength`), because the role is injected into the observer's system prompt on **every turn** — an unbounded role is a per-call token cost, not only a layout problem. Longest shipped persona role is 239 (Aristotle) → 761 chars of headroom. Counter sits **top-right of the Role label row** so it does not move when the box grows: muted, amber at 90%, red at cap. **A stray Enter used to produce an unparseable file** (`role: "line one` + raw newline + `line two"`, which YAML cannot hold and our parser then rejected, losing the save): `oneLine()` folds whitespace in the **emitter** (the guarantee for every caller) and in the webview. **Gotcha recorded in HANDOFF:** `webview.ts` is one giant template literal, so `\s` written there compiles to `/s+/` — matching the letter *s*, doing nothing, and invisible to `tsc`; correct form is `\\s`. | 2026-09-01 | (this commit) | M4.1 | D37a follow-up to D37: three corrections raised during review. (1) **Catalog-only trip guard**: auto-compaction trip math extracted into pure `shouldAutoCompact(estimate, maxContext, threshold)` — `maxContext` MUST come from `maxContextFor(bare)` (catalog), never from a `/models` payload. Discovered values are informational in the header caption only; trusting them for trip timing risked firing on stale OpenRouter/LiteLLM windows. Unknown models fall back to `UNKNOWN_CONTEXT_FLOOR` (8192). (2) **Model-aware summary cap**: `compactMaxTokensFor(model)` replaces the hard-coded 1024. Formula `clamp(256, maxOutputFor(bare) / 4, 2048)` — Haiku 3.5 (8192/4=2048) and Sonnet 4.5 (64000/4→capped 2048) both land on 2048, but a hypothetical 512-output model stays usable at 256. (3) **Ornith removed from the catalog** — it was a local-only model carried over from a prior in-tree comment; not part of the shipped catalog. | 2026-08-26 | (this commit) | M4/fix ×2 | (1) Approve-all deadlock (founder trial: run froze forever right after clicking "approved for session" — no done, no error). The 'approve-all' handler cached the tool and sent the notice but never resolved the waiting pendingApproval promise, so the loop hung at `await approve(...)`. Fix: resolve it (true). (2) maxTokens made model-aware: catalog gained per-model `maxOutput` (haiku 8192 / sonnet 64000 / opus 32768 / gpt-4o 16384 / o4-mini 100000); loop clamps its 16384 default via new `maxOutputFor()` (unknown/discovered models → conservative 8192, since a too-high request is a hard 400 while a too-low one only truncates, which stopReason now surfaces). Without the clamp, 0.7.6's flat 16384 would 400 every turn on Haiku 3.5. Version 0.7.7 | `tsc -b` clean; 85/85 vitest (2 new clamp tests: haiku clamps to 8192, sonnet keeps 16384); `node --check` inline PASS; bundle→package→install @0.7.7; markers in installed dist/extension.js (maxOutputFor ×3, resolve?.(true) ×1) |
| 2026-08-26 | (this commit) | M4/fix | Silent answer loss on truncation (founder trials 0.7.4/0.7.5: runs ended "done" with no final report). Root cause: loop hard-coded maxTokens 4096; canonical TurnResponse had no stopReason, so both adapters discarded the provider's truncation signal; and any no-tool-call turn was treated as 'complete' — a turn truncated mid-thinking (zero text, zero tool calls) ended the run as a normal "✓ done". Fix: stopReason added to the provider contract and mapped in AnthropicNative (stop_reason) + OpenAICompat (finish_reason='length'); loop raised maxTokens to 16384; a truncated no-tool turn now ends as reason 'cap' with a visible "[plato] output hit the token cap; the answer may be incomplete." note (same rendering path as the turn-cap note). Deferred: auto-continuation of truncated turns.
